- **The strongest case for:** ⚠️ if political decisions harm third parties, and if competence demonstrably improves outcomes, then distributing power without regard to competence imposes avoidable harm.
- **The strongest case against:** ⚠️ unequal political power expresses a public judgment that some citizens' standing is lesser, which insults equal citizenship; and the proposal is self-defeating in practice, because the criterion of competence must itself be set by someone, and that authority is exactly what is in dispute.
- **Why it stays optional:** ✅ it is an advanced defence and critique, not a syllabus term, and the owned parts are answerable through the competence/identification distinction already given in Session 3.

### A3. Republican anti-domination
A rival account of what democracy is for. Freedom on this view is not simply the absence of interference but the absence of **domination** — of standing subjection to another's arbitrary power, even where that power is not exercised.

- **Why it is attractive here:** it explains the discomfort with benevolent absolutism and with symbolic monarchy that the interference-based account cannot capture, since a subject may be entirely unmolested and still live at another's discretion.
- **The liberal rival:** rights and procedures already protect against arbitrary interference, and adding "domination" multiplies criteria without adding protection.
- **Boundary:** ✅ liberty itself is owned by *Social and Political Ideals*. Admit anti-domination here only as a bridge between liberty and the form of government, and only in a 15- or 20-marker.

### A4. Deliberation beyond the promoted core

Deliberative democracy was **promoted to Core on 15 August 2026** and is taught in Session 18 as the standard reply to the majoritarian objection. What remains optional is the **systemic and institutional design** question: how deliberation is to be organised at scale — deliberative forums, citizens' assemblies, structured consultation, sequencing between deliberation and voting — and whether a "deliberative system" can be assessed as a whole even when no single site within it is fully deliberative.

⚠️ **Named for balance:** Nadia Urbinati's work on the disfigurement of democratic representation is the standard optional route into this question; use it only where the stem concerns the *quality* of representation rather than the printed forms.

### A5. The strongest additional objection and reply
- **Objection.** Epistemic defences of democracy can quietly slide into rule by experts and revive Plato's suspicion of ordinary citizens. If democracy is justified because it produces better decisions, then evidence that some other arrangement produces still better decisions would justify abandoning it.
- **Reply.** The strongest democratic answer keeps expertise **advisory and contestable** while preserving equal standing in authorising power: competence may inform rule, but may not replace political equality. The epistemic defence is therefore best stated as a claim about **procedures of correction**, which presupposes equal standing, rather than as a claim about outcome quality, which does not.

### A6. Safe Indian deployment in a 15- or 20-marker
Use public reasoning, minority inclusion, media manipulation, federal diversity and institutional checks as examples of why democracy is more than periodic voting. ✅ Keep the example **analytic, not event-driven**: cite the constitutional or statutory item, classify it accurately, and return immediately to the argument. ❌ Do not characterise any Indian party, government, leader or period as populist, illiberal or propagandist, and do not treat a legal provision as evidence for a philosophical conclusion.

### A7. Promotion trigger and source trail
- **Fired and executed:** the deliberative justification of democracy was promoted to Core on **15 August 2026** and now sits in Session 18.
- **Still to promote if a PYQ asks it directly:** epistocracy, public reason as a named doctrine, or republican democracy.
- **Credible source trail:** Plato, *Republic*; Aristotle, *Politics*; J. S. Mill, *Considerations on Representative Government*; Jürgen Habermas, *Between Facts and Norms*; Nadia Urbinati, *Democracy Disfigured*; David Estlund, *Democratic Authority*; Philip Pettit, *Republicanism*. ⚠️ Cited by author and title only; no page, chapter, edition or verbatim wording is asserted for any of them.

## CONSOLIDATED REGISTER NOTES

> Compressed recall for the whole clause, in thirteen parts. This is **not** a second reading of the teaching: it prioritises definitions, classifications, exact provenance, causal logic, comparisons, traps, PYQ routes and answer frameworks. Everything below covers Sessions 1–20; the Optional Advanced block is deliberately excluded except where a line marks it as optional.

### A. Say it exactly — the definition bank

| Term | The sentence to reproduce |
|---|---|
| **Form of government** | The standing arrangement that answers four questions at once — who rules, by what title, for whose good, and under what limits. |
| **Monarchy** | Government by one ruler, usually occupying office by heredity; in ancient and medieval thought a natural symbol of unity. |
| **Absolute monarchy** | The monarch rules directly, concentrating executive, legislative and sometimes judicial functions, with weak institutional accountability. |
| **Constitutional monarchy** | The monarch reigns but elected institutions govern; accountability shifts to cabinet and parliament. |
| **Divine Right of Kings** | The monarch rules by God's will and is answerable primarily to God, not to the people. |
| **Theocracy** | A form of government in which political authority is claimed in the name of God, divine law, or authorised interpreters of sacred truth — a polity whose ultimate sovereignty is held to be divine rather than popular. |
| **Secularism** | The requirement that the state not be founded on the supremacy of one religious truth for all citizens; it protects equality of citizenship, liberty of conscience and coexistence under plural faith. |
| **Democracy** | Rule of the people, implying not only majority rule but also political equality, participation, accountability, public justification and some protection of rights. |
| **Liberal democracy** | Popular rule combined with constitutional limits, rights, rule of law and minority protection, so that the majority governs without converting number into unrestricted power. |
| **Procedural democracy** | Elections, competition, voting and rules: *are rulers chosen fairly?* |
| **Substantive democracy** | Justice, rights, inclusion and real equality: *do people actually govern under fair social conditions?* |
| **Polity (Aristotle)** | The mixed constitution: rule of the many under law, moderated by property, virtue and civic participation, resting on a large middle stratum. |
| **Power / authority (Weber)** | Power is the ability to secure compliance despite resistance; authority is power regarded as rightful by those subject to it. |
| **Ideal type** | An analytical exaggeration against which real mixtures are measured — diagnostic, never a description of a pure case. |
| **Routinisation of charisma** | The conversion of personally grounded authority into traditional or legal-rational form through inheritance, designation or institutionalisation into office. |
| **Iron law of oligarchy** | The tendency of every large organisation, including avowedly democratic ones, toward rule by a small leadership — defensibly a resistible tendency, not a law. |
| **Thin-centred ideology** | An ideology carrying no full programme of its own, which attaches to a host ideology — Mudde's characterisation of populism. |
| **Exclusive moral representation** | The claim that only one leader or movement represents the real people, so that rivals become enemies of the people — Müller's criterion. |
| **Illiberal democracy** | An electoral majority governing after the constraints that make electoral defeat survivable have been weakened, captured or bypassed. |
| **Manufactured consent** | Consent produced where access to information, exposure to rival argument and freedom from manipulation have been degraded. |
| **Regulator's dilemma** | Every content-based remedy for propaganda hands the state power over public truth — the power a propagandising state abuses. |

### B. The four classificatory axes — open every answer here

1. **Who rules?** one · few · many.
2. **For whose good?** the common interest · a sectional interest.
3. **By what title?** heredity and tradition · conquest · divine sanction · revelation and sacred law · consent and political equality.
4. **Under what limits?** law · rights · opposition · review · realistic replaceability · none.

- Keep **description** apart from **evaluation**: description records who holds power and how office is filled; evaluation judges whether the arrangement deserves obedience.
- **Aristotle fuses them:** number (descriptive) × common good or self-interest (normative) → six forms, three pairs of twins.
- **Fix the title first**; the characteristic danger follows from it — arbitrariness from heredity, crushed conscience from revelation, majoritarianism from number.

### C. Classical grid — Plato's descent and Aristotle's six forms

**Plato's degeneration:** aristocracy of knowledge → **timocracy** (honour) → **oligarchy** (wealth; the city splits rich/poor) → **democracy** (freedom and equality of desires) → **tyranny** (one demagogue). **Engine:** the ordering principle slips from reason to appetite; the city is the soul written large. ❓ Whether this is a historical law or an idealised model is disputed — assert the model, note the dispute.

**Plato's three arguments against democracy:** (1) rule is a craft requiring knowledge — the **ship-of-state analogy**; (2) democracy mistakes liberty for qualification, levelling knowledge and ignorance; (3) unregulated liberty makes restraint look oppressive, producing the demagogue who becomes tyrant. **Correction:** this is not a defence of hereditary monarchy — guardians are selected by training. **Democratic reply on four axes:** competence, freedom, desire, unity.

**Aristotle's grid:**

| Number | Right form (common good) | Deviant form (private interest) |
|---|---|---|
| One | Monarchy | Tyranny |
| Few | Aristocracy | Oligarchy |
| Many | **Polity** | Democracy (classical, deviant sense) |

- Members of a pair are **structurally identical** and differ only in purpose — so no institutional description delivers a normative verdict.
- Number tracks class: oligarchy = the wealthy few, democracy = the poor many. Hence **polity** and the middle stratum.
- **Polity is a precursor, not an identity:** Aristotle excluded most residents from citizenship; and polity argues **stability**, not justice.
- **The contrast formula:** Plato asks who ought ideally to rule; Aristotle asks what constitution can sustain a good political life under real social conditions.

### D. Monarchy — the split, the case, the objections, the two owned verdicts

- **The split that decides everything:** absolute (concentrates functions; weak accountability; efficient but normatively risky) against constitutional (reigns but does not govern; accountability shifts to cabinet and parliament; acceptable as symbolic headship).
- **For:** unity and continuity · decision and stability against faction · the above-politics symbol.
- **Against:** **equality** (office closed to equal claimants) · **merit** (birth guarantees neither wisdom nor virtue) · **accountability** (concentration invites arbitrariness) · **freedom** (survives only where law, rights and institutions restrain the crown).
- **The asymmetry:** defences are prudential and symbolic, objections are normative — usefulness is not entitlement.
- **The merit→removability shift:** the strong objection is not that monarchs are individually worse but that no regular non-violent procedure exists to act on the finding that one is.
- **2021 verdict:** monarchy can **coexist** with individual freedom only when constitutionalised and politically limited; as a principle of rule it does not **ground** freedom.
- **2023 verdict:** systematic only where "above politics" means **symbolic neutrality within a constitutional order**, not supra-legal power. Two senses: above party conflict (compatible) · above constitutional accountability (destroys the system).
- **Weber:** monarchy is the paradigm of **traditional** authority; **routinisation** explains why hereditary succession is systematic — and why that solves succession, not accountability.

### E. Theocracy — features, the interpretation problem, the two owned verdicts

- **Five features:** sovereignty of God · sacred law guiding or determining civil law · clerical mediation · limited pluralism (dissent constrained as theological deviance) · fusion of religious and political legitimacy.
- **Core claim:** norms are **received**, not created — which places rulers under a higher law they cannot rewrite.
- **The structural defect:** divine law must still be applied, so the **interpreter** acquires political power insulated from criticism, since objection to him can be recast as impiety. It arises even where every interpreter is sincere.
- **The line that decides every stem:** religiously informed public reasoning (compatible) against **theocratic sovereignty** — constitutive political authority for religious law (not compatible). The **dissenter test**: is disagreement ordinary dissent, or impiety?
- **Four-point tension with secularism:** privileges one framework · constrains dissent and apostasy · makes citizenship asymmetrical · narrows the public basis of law from shared reason to sectarian authority.
- **2019 verdict (status):** lacks constitutional centrality; retains three importances — historical form of legitimacy, living challenge to secular constitutionalism, reminder of conscience and sacred-law claims; **residual or contested status, not a governing norm**.
- **2025 verdict (validity):** name the test — consent, equal citizenship, public reason, revisability. **Internal validity for believers**; failure as a **general** form in a plural society. Two-sided close: defective on consent and equal citizenship; correct that purely procedural politics lacks a moral limit on rulers — which **constitutional rights** can supply without confessional sovereignty.
- **India (illustration only):** Articles 25–28 guarantee freedom of conscience and religion subject to public order, morality and health, and prohibit religious instruction in wholly State-funded institutions. ❌ No Indian actor is characterised.

### F. Divine right — the bridge and the three-step answer

- **Doctrine:** the monarch rules by God's will and answers primarily to God, not the people; historically it **sacralised** authority and strengthened absolutism.
- **The formula:** *divine right makes monarchy theologically legitimated; theocracy makes theology politically constitutive.*
- **Answerability chains:** divine-right monarchy → God → **king** → subjects. Theocracy → God → sacred law → **interpreters** → subjects.
- **Characteristic crises differ:** monarchy breaks at succession and personal conduct; theocracy breaks at interpretation and rival interpreters.
- **2022 three-step answer:** (1) **not necessary** — conceptually distinct; (2) **not unrelated** — historically intersecting, bridged by divine right; (3) **direction matters** — divine right sacralises kingship, theocracy relocates sovereignty. ❓ Concede that the distinction weakens where ruler and chief interpreter coincide.

### G. Democracy — models, design axes and minority protection

- **Two prior distinctions:** direct (participation; poor scaling) against representative (scale; distance); procedural (fairly chosen rulers) against substantive (fair social conditions of rule).
- **Representation tools:** delegate · trustee · mandate; descriptive against substantive representation.

| Model | Claim | Strength | Pathology |
|---|---|---|---|
| Majoritarian | number decides under political equality | authorisation, decisiveness | tyranny of the majority |
| Representative | agents authorised for limited periods | scale, specialised deliberation | distance, weak mandate control, elite capture |
| Liberal | majority rule bounded by rights, law, opposition, review | defeat survivable; dissent protected | formal safeguards with social exclusion |
| Participatory | action beyond periodic voting | civic education, local accountability | fatigue, unequal voice, over-mobilisation |
| Deliberative | public reason-giving, not only aggregation | revisability, mutual justification | demanding conditions of time and equality |

- **Design axes (secondary, trade-offs only):** parliamentary/presidential (responsibility and removability against stability and deadlock) · unitary/federal (uniformity against autonomy) · fusion/separation (coordination against prevention of concentration) · centralisation/decentralisation (uniform standards against participation and subsidiarity). **Control:** can each organ explain, contest and correct the others without paralysis? **India:** parliamentary + holding-together federal under constitutional supremacy and judicial review; the Constitution (Seventy-third and Seventy-fourth Amendment) Acts, **1992**, inserted Parts IX and IX-A.
- **Minority protection (2018, 2020):** constitutive, not generosity. **Achievements** (institutional): legal guarantees, representation, judicial review, civil liberties, protected dissent, constitutional accommodation. **Limits** (social): prejudice outlasting formal equality, majoritarian electoral incentive, formal toleration with substantive exclusion. **Verdict:** committed normatively; success depends on constitutional culture, social equality and institutional independence.
- **Liberty and equality (2018):** held in **institutionalised tension** — liberty without equality entrenches domination, equality without liberty flattens dissent. **Distinctiveness** lies not in possessing them but in institutionalising **equal authorisation** and **peaceful replacement**. **Ambedkar:** political equality cannot long survive amidst deep social and economic inequality.
- **Deliberation and epistemics:** **Habermas** — legitimacy from communication approximating freedom, reciprocity and absence of domination; **epistemic defence** — not that opinion tracks truth but that procedures allow error-detection, revision and access to dispersed knowledge. **Expertise under democratic control:** authorise goals, demand reasons, hold experts accountable.
- **Three bounded anchors, one job each:** Rousseau — representation is incomplete; Mill — participation educates (with a competence-weighting residue ❓); Tocqueville — majority rule needs associations, local self-government, free press and review.

### H. Legitimacy and elite competition — Weber, Schumpeter, Michels

| Type | Ground | Administration | Succession | Affinity |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Traditional | sanctity of immemorial custom | patrimonial staff, household officials | inheritance or custom | monarchy, hereditary theocracy |
| Charismatic | devotion to an exceptional person | personally chosen disciples | acute — inherently unstable | revolutionary, prophetic, plebiscitary rule |
| Legal-rational | belief in the legality of enacted rules | bureaucracy: offices, jurisdictions, files, career | impersonally, by rule | the modern constitutional state |

- **Presupposition:** legitimacy is **sociological belief**, not normative justification. ❌ Never equate "legitimate" with "justified".
- **Routinisation:** charisma must convert into traditional or legal-rational form to survive — movement → party → bureaucracy.
- **The crossing that earns depth:** a democracy is normally legal-rational yet may be captured by a plebiscitary-charismatic leader while retaining its legal forms.
- **Schumpeter:** democracy is the **competitive struggle for the people's vote**; the people **produce a government**, not govern. Premises: no determinate common good; no determinate popular will; weak citizen grasp of remote issues. Strength: the irreducible minimum — an open, uncertain, repeatable contest. Objection: deflationary; cannot distinguish a deliberative election from a well-managed one.
- **Michels, *Political Parties* (1911):** scale, specialisation and control of information entrench a permanent leadership — "who says organisation says oligarchy". Defensible form: a **resistible tendency**, since design demonstrably alters outcomes.
- **The sequence that makes an argument:** Plato objects on **competence** → Michels on **organisation** → Schumpeter **concedes both** and redefines democracy so neither is fatal.

### I. Democratic decay — populism, illiberal democracy, propaganda, six pathologies

- **Three contested accounts:** **Mudde (2004)** thin-centred ideology, pure people against corrupt elite, attaches to a host ideology · **Müller (2016)** exclusive moral representation; **anti-elitism is compatible with democracy, anti-pluralism is not** · **Laclau (2005)** a logic of articulation, potentially democratising. ❌ Never present one as settled.
- **The six-step slide:** representation deficit → a leader claims to embody the real people → disagreement becomes betrayal → checking institutions recast as elite obstruction → weakened, captured or bypassed → elections continue without survivable defeat.
- **Controlling distinction:** majority rule is a decision procedure; constitutional democracy is majority rule **plus** entrenched rights, independent adjudication and secured conditions for the next contest.
- **Form/substance grid:** elections/fairness of contest · majority rule/limits on majorities · courts/independence · press/plurality of ownership · opposition/realistic alternation.
- **Propaganda in six steps:** authorisation expresses judgment → judgment is one's own only under access, exposure and freedom from manipulation → concentrated communication power degrades all three → consent is **manufactured** → sociological legitimacy without normative authorisation → democratic quality depends on the **conditions of opinion-formation**.
- **The regulator's dilemma and structural remedies:** plural ownership · transparency of political finance and paid messaging · independent electoral adjudication · protected professional journalism · civic education · rights of reply. **India:** Article 19(1)(a) with reasonable restrictions under Article 19(2); the Representation of the People Act, **1951**. ⚠️ Enactment is not enforcement.

| Pathology | Mechanism | Reply | Residue |
|---|---|---|---|
| Tyranny of the majority | number becomes unlimited authority | entrenched rights, review, federal protection | checks look like obstruction |
| Elite capture | organisation, money, expertise, information | competition, transparency, alternation | counter-elites circulate |
| Short-termism | electoral incentives favour immediate gain | scrutiny, independent institutions, intergenerational duties | insulation weakens control |
| Populist anti-pluralism | exclusive moral representation | protected opposition, plural media, fair contest | "populism" as a partisan label |
| Manufactured consent | agenda control degrades opinion-formation | structural plurality, transparency, education | content regulation empowers the state over truth |
| Bureaucratic domination | expertise and files displace representation | reasons, review, oversight, decentralisation | specialised administration is indispensable |

### J. The eight comparison axes — the verdict engine

Run **all three forms** down each axis; never write three mini-essays.

1. Source of authority · 2. Type of legitimacy claimed (Weber) · 3. Mode of succession or selection · 4. Accountability · 5. Relation to law · 6. Treatment of dissent · 7. Equality of citizenship · 8. **Capacity to correct error**.

- **Monarchy on axis 8:** prudent counsel and customary restraint; no regular non-violent procedure for acting on incompetence.
- **Theocracy on axis 8:** hardest — the norm is unamendable and the interpreter insulated, so dissent is reclassified rather than answered.
- **Democracy on axis 8:** institutionalised through opposition, elections, courts and public criticism — but **conditional**, since form can survive substance.
- Axis 8 is the axis candidates most often omit and the one that most often decides.

### K. Directive decoder and answer methods

| Directive | Compulsory structural move | Failure mode |
|---|---|---|
| **Comment on** | claim → argument → one objection → verdict | drifting into general description |
| **Discuss** | doctrine → rival → objection → reply → verdict | three disconnected paragraphs |
| **Critically examine / evaluate** | two objections, each with a reply and a residual problem | a criticism list with no defence attempted |
| **Can X be accepted / Is X valid?** | fix the test → apply → verdict | asserting acceptability with no standard |
| **Does X leave room for Y?** | what X entails → what Y requires → can they co-exist | listing historical examples instead of arguing |
| **How far are X and Y distinctive of Z?** | show what Z has that rivals lack; concede what rivals secure | proving possession, not distinctiveness |
| **Compare / distinguish forms** | fix 5–8 shared axes, run all forms down each, then adjudicate | sequential mini-essays |

- **10 marks (~150–220 words, 4 moves, ~12 min):** exact target → argument spine as numbered premises → one evidence unit with its limitation → graded verdict, usually asymmetric. ❌ Do not run the authority typology, the populism debate and the classical grid together.
- **15 marks (~250–330 words, 6 moves, ~18 min):** frame the axis of dispute → strongest case for, charitably → strongest case against on the same axes → one table → one worked objection → reply → residual problem → conditional verdict.
- **20 marks (~330–400 words, 8 moves, ~25 min):** provisional thesis in the stem's own verb → classificatory placement on **both** grids → strongest case for with its presupposition → strongest rival → two objection–reply chains ending in residual problems → the form/substance or routinisation analysis → one dated Indian illustration, classified → graded verdict conceding something to the losing side.

**Graded verdict formulas — adapt, never reproduce mechanically:**

- **Asymmetric:** "Plato's diagnosis of demagoguery and defective leadership-selection survives; his authoritarian remedy does not, because it supplies no mechanism of consent or of correcting the guardians' own error."
- **Distinctiveness:** "Liberty and equality are not unique to democracy in their formal statement, but democracy alone institutionalises the equal authorisation of rule and the peaceful replacement of rulers."
- **Criterion (theocracy):** "Judged by consent, equal citizenship and revisability, theocracy is normatively defective; judged by moral limitation of the ruler, it identifies a real deficiency in purely procedural politics."
- **Form/substance:** "The regime retains democratic form; the question is whether it retains the conditions — fair contest, protected minority, independent adjudication, realistic alternation — that make the form worth having."
- **Cross-typology:** "Classification by number of rulers tells us who decides; classification by type of legitimacy tells us why they are obeyed. A complete assessment of any form requires both."
- **Dilemma (propaganda):** "Democracy survives propaganda through contestable institutions rather than through censorship, because every content-based remedy hands the state precisely the power over public truth that a propagandising state abuses."

### L. Trap list and PYQ ownership (2018–2025)

**Final pre-submission check — fourteen traps:**

| ❌ Trap | ✅ Correction |
|---|---|
| Monarchy means every monarch has unlimited power | distinguish absolute, limited and constitutional monarchy |
| Theocracy means religion influences politics | theocracy makes divine law or authorised interpreters the governing source of political authority |
| Democracy is simply periodic election | distinguish electoral, liberal, participatory, deliberative and substantive democracy |
| Majority rule is unlimited | constitutional democracy protects rights and minority standing |
| Plato merely "hated freedom" | reconstruct the competence, order and demagoguery arguments before criticising |
| Aristotle's polity is identical with modern democracy | it is a mixed-government precursor, not universal-suffrage democracy |
| Secularism requires exclusion of every religious voice | it concerns equal citizenship and the institutional relation of state and religion |
| Expertise and democracy are opposites | the issue is the authorisation and accountability of expertise |
| Dictatorship, authoritarianism and totalitarianism are synonyms | concentration · suppression of contestation · comprehensive penetration |
| Parliamentary means democratic and presidential means authoritarian | both can be democratic or not; the distinction concerns executive–legislative relation and tenure |
| Federalism or decentralisation is democracy by itself | territorial distribution still needs rights, accountability and contestation |
| Separation of powers means no institutional interaction | modern systems combine differentiated competence with checks and review |
| Bureaucracy is merely neutral expertise | administration can become unaccountable domination without reasons, oversight and correction |
| "Legitimate" in Weber means "justified" | legitimacy there is belief about rightfulness; the normative question is untouched |

**Provenance the paper can punish — learn these, invent nothing else:** Michels, *Political Parties*, **1911** · Mudde, "The Populist Zeitgeist", *Government and Opposition*, **2004** · Laclau, *On Populist Reason*, **2005** · Müller, *What Is Populism?*, **2016** · Representation of the People Act, **1951** · Constitution (Seventy-third and Seventy-fourth Amendment) Acts, **1992**, Parts IX and IX-A · Articles 19(1)(a) and 19(2) · Articles 25–28.

**PYQ ownership — exactly 10 owner parts, and the route for each:**

| Year | Part | Marks | Demand | Route |
|---|---|---:|---|---|
| 2018 | Q1(a) | 10 | Liberal democracy; deeper principles for social cohesion; Indian context | Sessions 16, 17 |
| 2019 | Q1(b) | 10 | Status of theocracy in the modern secular state | Sessions 12, 13 |
| 2020 | Q1(b) | 10 | How far do liberal democracies safeguard minorities? | Session 16 |
| 2021 | Q1(d) | 10 | Does monarchy leave room for individual freedom? | Sessions 7, 10 |
| 2022 | Q4(a) | 20 | Propaganda as a challenge to democratic government | Session 20 |
| 2022 | Q4(c) | 15 | Are monarchy and theocracy necessarily related? Divine Right | Session 9 |
| 2023 | Q1(e) | 10 | Monarchs above politics — a systematic form? | Session 10 |
| 2023 | Q4(b) | 15 | Challenges faced by a democratic state and remedies | Sessions 19, 20 |
| 2025 | Q1(d) | 10 | Comment on Plato's critique of Democracy | Sessions 2, 3 |
| 2025 | Q4(b) | 15 | Can theocracy be accepted as a valid form? | Session 13 |

⚠️ **2024 carries no owned part** in this clause. The practical revision target is therefore 2018–2023 and 2025, and the internal split is democracy five, monarchy three, theocracy three, with 2022 Q4(c) counted once under both monarchy and theocracy.

```text
  the GOOD many-form is POLITY
  -> "Aristotle classified governments into monarchy, aristocracy and democracy" COLLAPSES
     the purpose axis and destroys the grid
  -> reading his "democracy" as the MODERN constitutional kind is the standing ANACHRONISM
        |
        v
NUMBER TRACKS CLASS -- WHY THE GRID BECOMES A SOCIAL ANALYSIS
  OLIGARCHY -> in practice the rule of the WEALTHY few
  DEMOCRACY -> in practice the rule of the POOR many
  -> hence the corrective is not a better COUNT but a MIDDLE STRATUM (Panel 5)
        |
        v
LIMIT ? -> "common advantage" is NOT self-specifying and needs an independent theory of the
           good; it is checkable only INDIRECTLY, through whether those affected can CONTEST
           decisions and whether ERROR can be CORRECTED
ANSWER USE -> opens any classification stem; fixes the shared axes for any comparison stem

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 5/24: Polity as the middle course, and the limit of the modern analogy
THE TWO EXTREMES POLITY IS BUILT AGAINST
  OLIGARCHY: rule of the WEALTHY few in their own interest -> exclusion, class domination
  DEMOCRACY (deviant): rule of the POOR many in their own interest -> faction, instability
        \                                                                      /
         v                                                                    v
POLITY = THE MIXED CONSTITUTION
  * rule of the MANY under LAW
  * moderated by PROPERTY, VIRTUE and CIVIC PARTICIPATION
  * resting on a large MIDDLE stratum, because extremes of wealth and poverty produce
    FACTIONAL rule
        |
        v
A MIDDLE COURSE IN THREE DIRECTIONS AT ONCE
  against STATISM ......... does not absorb all value into one all-wise ruler or total state
  against INDIVIDUALISM ... does not reduce politics to private pursuit or unrestricted
                            self-assertion
  for CIVIC MODERATION .... embeds citizenship in a LAWFUL order directed to the COMMON GOOD
        |
        v
THE CONTRAST FORMULA -- THE MOST EFFICIENT SENTENCE IN THE CLASSICAL MATERIAL
  PLATO asks -> WHO OUGHT IDEALLY TO RULE?
  ARISTOTLE asks -> WHAT CONSTITUTION CAN SUSTAIN A GOOD POLITICAL LIFE UNDER REAL SOCIAL
                    CONDITIONS?
        |
        v
THE MODERN ANALOGY AND ITS EXACT LIMIT
  SIMILAR -> modern constitutional democracy also combines POPULAR AUTHORISATION with LIMITS,
             INSTITUTIONS, RIGHTS and CHECKS, and distrusts both wealth-rule and unrestrained
             numerical majority
  DIFFERENT -> Aristotle EXCLUDED many residents from CITIZENSHIP; modern democracy claims
             UNIVERSAL EQUAL citizenship
  THEREFORE -> an illuminating MIXED-GOVERNMENT PRECURSOR, NOT universal-suffrage democracy
        |
        v
LIMIT ? -> polity is defended as a SOCIOLOGICAL claim about STABILITY, not a demonstration of
           JUSTICE. A durable regime built on exclusion satisfies stability and fails justice,
           and the grid cannot detect that defect from inside itself.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 6/24: Regime families -- the grammar that locates the printed three
CONSTITUTIONAL GOVERNMENT
  rulers exercise LIMITED powers under general LAW, RIGHTS, REVIEW and procedures of REMOVAL
  TEST -> a constitution in NAME is insufficient if the limits CANNOT BE ENFORCED
        |  ---------------- the limits fail ----------------
        v
ABSOLUTE GOVERNMENT
  the ruler or ruling body claims FINAL power, NOT institutionally answerable to ordinary
  subjects
  TEST -> DECISIVENESS supplies neither LEGITIMACY nor ERROR-CORRECTION
        |
        v
DICTATORSHIP
  decision and coercive power CONCENTRATED without regular COMPETITIVE accountability
  may be PERSONAL, MILITARY, PARTY-BASED or EMERGENCY in origin; the common feature is
  UNANSWERABLE CONCENTRATION
        |
        v
AUTHORITARIANISM
  opposition, public discussion and accountability SHARPLY RESTRICTED, though SOME social or
  institutional plurality may SURVIVE
  TEST -> it need NOT seek control of every belief or social sphere
        |
        v
TOTALITARIANISM
  a ruling IDEOLOGY and PARTY-STATE seek COMPREHENSIVE mobilisation and PENETRATION of
  political AND social life
  TEST -> more ambitious than ordinary dictatorship or authoritarian closure
        |
        v
THE THREE-WORD FORMULA THAT EARNS THE MARK
  DICTATORSHIP ...... names CONCENTRATION of decision
  AUTHORITARIANISM .. names SUPPRESSION of contestation
  TOTALITARIANISM ... names PENETRATION of the whole of social life
  they are NOT synonyms; real regimes are MIXTURES that CHANGE OVER TIME
        |
        v
SOURCE CONTROL -> Gauba, local searchable PDF pp. 510-514, supplies the democracy/
  authoritarianism contrast and the dictatorship setting; the authoritarian/totalitarian
  distinction is a comparative-theory reconstruction associated with LINZ and ARENDT and is
  NOT a new printed syllabus limb
FENCE -> fascist, communist and ideological HISTORIES -> Political Ideologies, World History

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 7/24: Monarchy -- definition and the split that decides every stem
MONARCHY = government by ONE RULER, usually occupying office by HEREDITY; among the OLDEST
forms, and in ancient and medieval thought a NATURAL SYMBOL OF UNITY
        |
        +-------------------------------+-------------------------------+
        v                                                               v
ABSOLUTE MONARCHY                                       CONSTITUTIONAL MONARCHY
  CHARACTER: the monarch RULES DIRECTLY with              CHARACTER: the monarch REIGNS,
    concentrated authority                                  elected institutions GOVERN
  SOURCE: heredity, conquest, tradition,                  SOURCE: historic continuity PLUS
    divine right                                            constitutional LIMITATION
  ACCOUNTABILITY: WEAK institutional                      ACCOUNTABILITY SHIFTS to the
    accountability                                          CABINET and PARLIAMENT
  VERDICT: efficient but NORMATIVELY RISKY                VERDICT: acceptable as SYMBOLIC
                                                            HEADSHIP
        |
        v
WHAT EACH VARIANT DOES WITH POWER
  ABSOLUTE -> concentrates EXECUTIVE + LEGISLATIVE + sometimes JUDICIAL functions in ONE person
  CONSTITUTIONAL -> preserves the monarch as CEREMONIAL or INTEGRATING authority while REAL
                    political power lies with REPRESENTATIVE INSTITUTIONS and LAW
        |
        v
WHY THE SPLIT DECIDES THE PAPER
  2021 -> "does monarchy leave room for individual FREEDOM?"
  2023 -> "if monarchs are ABOVE POLITICS, can monarchy be SYSTEMATIC?"
  BOTH are answered mainly in the CONSTITUTIONAL variant. An answer treating monarchy as ONE
  undivided thing is either UNFAIR to limited monarchy or ABSURD about absolutism.
        |
        v
ON THE LEGITIMACY GRID (Panel 19) -> monarchy is the paradigm of TRADITIONAL AUTHORITY:
  sanctity of IMMEMORIAL CUSTOM | PATRIMONIAL staff and household officials | succession
  solved by INHERITANCE or CUSTOM
TRAP -> confusing HEREDITARY with ABSOLUTE. Heredity concerns HOW THE OFFICE IS FILLED;
        absoluteness concerns WHETHER THE OFFICE IS LIMITED.

ARGUMENTS FOR                                 OBJECTIONS AGAINST

 national identity
--------------------------------------------  ------------------------------------------------

 decisive, less paralysed by FACTION             Plato's philosopher-ruler is chosen by
 than assemblies                                 KNOWLEDGE and TRAINING, not heredity
--------------------------------------------  ------------------------------------------------

 in constitutional monarchies the               concentration of power in ONE person invites
 monarch is above PARTISAN competition:          ARBITRARINESS; where institutions are weak,

THE MONARCHICAL REPLY, AND ITS EXACT LIMIT
  REPLY -> constitutional monarchy can separate a POLITICALLY NEUTRAL SYMBOL of continuity
           from ELECTED government
  LIMIT -> this may defend CEREMONIAL monarchy, but NOT an absolute ruler's right to govern
           WITHOUT consent or accountability -- and it concedes the point by removing the
           monarch from government altogether
        |
        v
THE ASYMMETRY THAT IS THE VERDICT
  arguments FOR are PRUDENTIAL and SYMBOLIC (stability, continuity, unity, decisiveness)
  arguments AGAINST are NORMATIVE (equality, merit, accountability, freedom)
  -> the two sets NEVER MEET: usefulness is not entitlement
  -> monarchy survives less as a JUSTIFICATORY IDEAL than as a HISTORICALLY ADAPTED INSTITUTION
MERIT->REMOVABILITY SHIFT -> the strong form of the merit objection is NOT that monarchs are
  individually worse, but that NO REGULAR NON-VIOLENT PROCEDURE exists to act on the finding

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 9/24: Divine right -- the bridge, and the 2022 three-step answer
THE DOCTRINE OF THE DIVINE RIGHT OF KINGS
  the monarch rules by GOD'S WILL and is answerable PRIMARILY TO GOD, not to the people
  EFFECT -> historically STRENGTHENED ABSOLUTISM by SACRALISING political authority: if the
            king answers to God alone, NO EARTHLY FORUM may call him to account
        |
        +---------------------------------+---------------------------------+
        v                                                                   v
IN MONARCHY                                             IN THEOCRACY
  the ruler is a KING                                     sovereignty is EXPLICITLY vested in
  divine sanction may LEGITIMISE his office                GOD and INTERPRETED through clergy,
                                                           scripture or SACRED LAW
        |
        v
THE EXAM FORMULA -- WRITE IT EXACTLY
  DIVINE RIGHT makes MONARCHY *THEOLOGICALLY LEGITIMATED*
  THEOCRACY makes THEOLOGY *POLITICALLY CONSTITUTIVE*
        |
        v
THE ANSWERABILITY CHAINS -- THE DIAGRAM THAT SETTLES THE STEM
  DIVINE-RIGHT MONARCHY -> GOD -> KING -> SUBJECTS ......... the KING is decisive
  THEOCRACY ------------> GOD -> SACRED LAW -> INTERPRETERS -> SUBJECTS
                                              the INTERPRETER is decisive, because sacred law
                                              DOES NOT APPLY ITSELF
        |
        v
THE THREE-STEP ANSWER TO "NECESSARILY RELATED?" (2022 Q4(c))
  STEP 1  NOT NECESSARY -> conceptually distinct: one locates the TITLE in a PERSON sanctioned
          by God; the other locates SOVEREIGNTY ITSELF in God
  STEP 2  BUT NOT UNRELATED -> they have HISTORICALLY INTERSECTED, and divine right is the
          BRIDGE giving monarchy a THEOLOGICAL FOUNDATION
  STEP 3  DIRECTION MATTERS -> divine-right monarchy SECULARISES little; it SACRALISES
          KINGSHIP. Theocracy places sovereignty MORE DIRECTLY in God or religious law.
        |
        v
CHARACTERISTIC CRISES DIFFER -- THIS IS WHY THE DISTINCTION IS NOT VERBAL
  divine-right monarchy breaks at SUCCESSION and at the KING'S PERSONAL CONDUCT
  theocracy breaks at INTERPRETATION and at RIVAL INTERPRETERS
  ? CONCEDE -> where the ruler is ALSO the chief interpreter, the distinction WEAKENS
TRAP -> answering "yes" or "no"; the stem's operative word is NECESSARILY

FIX TWO VARIABLES FIRST -> the TYPE of monarchy and the MEANING of freedom
IN FAVOUR                                     AGAINST
  constitutional monarchy may coexist with      absolute monarchy SUBORDINATES subjects to
  CIVIL LIBERTIES, REPRESENTATIVE government    PERSONAL rule
  and RULE OF LAW                               even SYMBOLIC monarchy carries INHERITED
  if the monarch is SYMBOLIC, freedom is not    HIERARCHY and UNEQUAL CIVIC STATUS
  necessarily threatened by the institution     modern freedom REQUIRES accountability,
  itself                                        rights and EQUAL CITIZENSHIP -- none of which
                                                arises FROM HEREDITY AS SUCH
        |
        v
BALANCED CONCLUSION -> monarchy can COEXIST with individual freedom ONLY WHEN
CONSTITUTIONALISED AND POLITICALLY LIMITED; monarchy AS A PRINCIPLE OF RULE does not itself
GROUND freedom.   [ COEXIST is conceded | GROUND is refused ]
        |
        v
[2023 Q1(e)] "IF MONARCHS ARE ABOVE POLITICS, CAN MONARCHY BE SYSTEMATIC?"
SYSTEMATICITY REQUIRES -> principles of LEGITIMACY, CONTINUITY and ROLE CLARITY
  YES in the constitutional variant: the monarch's NON-PARTISAN role is DEFINED BY
  CONSTITUTIONAL CONVENTIONS, while ELECTED institutions perform GOVERNANCE
        |
        v
THE TWO SENSES OF "ABOVE POLITICS" -- THE WHOLE 2023 MARK
  SENSE A -> above PARTISAN COMPETITION .............. COMPATIBLE with a systematic order
  SENSE B -> above CONSTITUTIONAL ACCOUNTABILITY ..... DESTROYS the order it claims to head
TWO RESERVATIONS
  1. "above politics" means above PARTY CONFLICT, NOT above CONSTITUTIONAL ACCOUNTABILITY
  2. if monarchy becomes GENUINELY UNACCOUNTABLE, the system CEASES to be constitutional and

        |
        v
VERDICT -> monarchy is systematic only where "above politics" means SYMBOLIC NEUTRALITY
WITHIN A CONSTITUTIONAL ORDER, not SUPRA-LEGAL POWER
WEBERIAN REINFORCEMENT -> ROUTINISATION converts personal charisma into inherited or
  office-based authority, so HEREDITARY SUCCESSION is a SYSTEMATIC solution to SUCCESSION --
  but nothing in routinisation makes monarchy ACCOUNTABLE
FINAL ASSESSMENT -> strongest modern defence is PRUDENTIAL and SYMBOLIC; weakest point is
  NORMATIVE -- it sits uneasily with EQUALITY, MERIT and DEMOCRATIC LEGITIMACY

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 11/24: Theocracy -- definition, five features, sovereignty of God
DEFINITION -> a form of government in which POLITICAL AUTHORITY is claimed in the NAME OF GOD,
DIVINE LAW, or AUTHORISED INTERPRETERS of sacred truth
  NOT merely a society INFLUENCED by religion -- a polity whose ULTIMATE SOURCE OF SOVEREIGNTY
  is held to be DIVINE rather than POPULAR
        |
        v
THE FIVE ESSENTIAL FEATURES
  SOVEREIGNTY OF GOD ......... final authority belongs to DIVINE WILL, not the people
  SACRED LAW ................. religious law GUIDES or DETERMINES civil law
  CLERICAL MEDIATION ......... priests, jurists, clergy or religious elites INTERPRET divine
                               command
  LIMITED PLURALISM .......... dissent is CONSTRAINED if it appears as THEOLOGICAL DEVIANCE
  FUSION OF RELIGION/STATE ... political legitimacy and religious legitimacy OVERLAP
        |
        v
WHY THIS IS CONCEPTUALLY STRONGER THAN MERE PUBLIC RELIGIOSITY
  it denotes a CONSTITUTIONAL-MORAL ORDER grounded in REVELATION or SACRED AUTHORITY --
  a claim about the SOURCE and STRUCTURE of SOVEREIGNTY, not about how devout a population is
        |
        v
THE CORE CLAIM, STATED AT ITS STRONGEST
  human beings do NOT CREATE ultimate norms; they RECEIVE them. Political authority is
  legitimate only insofar as it CONFORMS TO DIVINE COMMAND.
  APPARENT ADVANTAGE -> it LIMITS THE ARROGANCE of human rulers by placing them UNDER A HIGHER
  LAW they cannot rewrite for their own convenience
        |
        v
THE PROBLEM GENERATED BY THE DOCTRINE'S OWN STRENGTH -> *WHO SPEAKS FOR GOD?*
  divine law DOES NOT APPLY ITSELF -> someone must say what it requires HERE AND NOW
  -> once interpretation is HUMAN, THEOLOGICAL CERTAINTY CAN MASK ORDINARY POWER STRUGGLES
  -> the INTERPRETER acquires political power INSULATED FROM CRITICISM, because objection to
     the interpreter can be RECAST AS OBJECTION TO THE SOURCE
  STRUCTURAL, NOT MORAL -> the difficulty arises EVEN WHERE EVERY INTERPRETER IS SINCERE
        |
        v
COMPARISON ? -> constitutions also require interpretation, but a constitutional interpreter
  operates under PUBLIC REASONS any citizen may CONTEST, may be OVERRIDDEN BY AMENDMENT, and
  may be criticised WITHOUT the criticism being reclassified as a DIFFERENT KIND OF OFFENCE
TRAP -> defining theocracy as "religion influencing politics" DISSOLVES the concept and makes
        both owned theocracy stems unanswerable

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 12/24: Theocracy against secularism -- the constitutive-authority line
THREE ARRANGEMENTS OFTEN CONFUSED -- KEEP THEM APART
  1. a DEMOCRACY may PERMIT religious citizens to bring MORAL VALUES into PUBLIC DEBATE
                                                                       -> COMPATIBLE
  2. a SECULAR STATE may ENGAGE religion through REFORM, ACCOMMODATION or EQUAL RESPECT
                                                                       -> COMPATIBLE
  3. a THEOCRACY gives CONSTITUTIVE POLITICAL AUTHORITY to divine law or its CLERICAL
     INTERPRETERS                                                      -> NOT COMPATIBLE
  -> theocracy is NOT the same as a MORALLY INFORMED POLITY; it is a specific claim about the
     SOURCE and STRUCTURE of SOVEREIGNTY
        |
        v
THE DISSENTER TEST -- ONE LINE THAT SEPARATES THEM
  under (1) and (2) disagreement is ORDINARY POLITICAL DISSENT and the religious argument must
  COMPETE with reasons others may CONTEST AS EQUALS
  under (3) disagreement is IMPIETY, because the SOURCE of the norm is placed BEYOND PUBLIC
  CHALLENGE
        |
        v
WHAT SECULARISM ACTUALLY ASKS
  that the state NOT be founded on the SUPREMACY OF ONE RELIGIOUS TRUTH for all citizens
  in modern constitutional terms it protects EQUALITY OF CITIZENSHIP, LIBERTY OF CONSCIENCE
  and PEACEFUL COEXISTENCE under conditions of PLURAL FAITH
  -> it does NOT require HOSTILITY to religion, nor the BANISHMENT of religious argument
        |
        v
THE FOUR-POINT STRUCTURAL TENSION
  theocracy PRIVILEGES one sacred framework .......... vs no established supremacy
  theocracy CONSTRAINS dissent and apostasy .......... vs liberty of conscience
  theocracy makes CITIZENSHIP ASYMMETRICAL ........... vs equal standing regardless of belief
  theocracy NARROWS the public basis of law from ..... vs law resting on reasons citizens of
  SHARED REASON to SECTARIAN AUTHORITY                  different faiths and none may contest
  -> sharpest in DIVERSE societies, where EQUAL RESPECT among religions and CONSTITUTIONAL
     MORALITY are central
        |
        v
INDIA-FACING ILLUSTRATION (dated, classified, non-empirical)
  ARTICLES 25-28 guarantee FREEDOM OF CONSCIENCE AND RELIGION, SUBJECT TO public order,
  morality and health, and PROHIBIT religious instruction in WHOLLY STATE-FUNDED institutions
  -> a DATED CONSTITUTIONAL FACT used as ILLUSTRATION; it PROVES no philosophical thesis, and
     NO Indian government, party, leader or period is characterised
? CONTESTED -> whether the constraint of PUBLIC REASON is genuinely NEUTRAL among traditions

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 13/24: Theocracy -- 2019 status against 2025 validity
[2019 Q1(b)] "STATUS OF THEOCRACY IN THE MODERN SECULAR STATE" -- A QUESTION ABOUT PLACE
  WHAT IS TRUE -> in a modern secular state theocracy LACKS CONSTITUTIONAL CENTRALITY; the
  dominant direction of modern politics runs toward POPULAR SOVEREIGNTY, RIGHTS, LEGAL
  EQUALITY and INSTITUTIONAL ACCOUNTABILITY
  BUT IT REMAINS PHILOSOPHICALLY IMPORTANT IN THREE WAYS
    1. as a HISTORICAL FORM OF LEGITIMACY against which modern constitutionalism defined itself
    2. as a LIVING CHALLENGE to secular constitutionalism -- it DENIES that consent can ground
       ultimate norms
    3. as a REMINDER that modern states still confront claims of CONSCIENCE, SACRED LAW and
       RELIGIOUS IDENTITY that rights alone do not dissolve
  JUDGMENT -> RESIDUAL or CONTESTED status, NOT a governing norm
        |
        v
[2025 Q4(b)] "CAN THEOCRACY BE ACCEPTED AS A VALID FORM?" -- A QUESTION ABOUT A TEST
  NAME THE TEST FIRST -> CONSENT | EQUAL CITIZENSHIP | PUBLIC REASON | REVISABILITY
IN FAVOUR                                     AGAINST
  MORAL UNITY and COLLECTIVE PURPOSE            NO EQUAL STANDING for citizens of different
  RESTRAINT on purely SELF-INTERESTED           faiths or no faith
  politics -- rulers sit under a law they       LIBERTY OF CONSCIENCE is compromised
  cannot rewrite                                DISSENT becomes IMPIETY, not legitimate
  STABLE ETHICAL ORDER derived from             disagreement
  SACRED OBLIGATION                             CLERICAL MEDIATION -> INTERPRETIVE MONOPOLY
                                                ADAPTATION to modern RIGHTS discourse is hard
        |
        v
VERDICT -> theocracy may claim INTERNAL VALIDITY FOR BELIEVERS, but as a GENERAL FORM OF
GOVERNMENT IN A PLURAL MODERN SOCIETY it FAILS the tests of EQUAL CITIZENSHIP, PUBLIC REASON
and ACCOUNTABILITY.
        |
        v
THE TWO-SIDED CRITERION VERDICT -- MEMORISE THE SHAPE
  judged by CONSENT, EQUAL CITIZENSHIP and REVISABILITY -> NORMATIVELY DEFECTIVE
  judged by MORAL LIMITATION OF THE RULER -> it identifies a REAL DEFICIENCY in purely
  procedural politics
  RELIGIOUS ETHICS may ENRICH public reason; THEOCRATIC SOVEREIGNTY cannot satisfy EQUAL
  CITIZENSHIP
OBJECTION/REPLY -> "applying liberal tests BEGS THE QUESTION" / the INTERNAL vs GENERAL
  distinction avoids the circle: it is the PLURALITY of the society, not the critic's premises,
  that defeats theocracy as a GENERAL form
FINAL ASSESSMENT -> the STRONGEST RIVAL to secular democratic legitimacy, because it relocates
  sovereignty OUTSIDE HUMAN CONSENT; its promise of MORAL UNITY costs PLURALISM, EQUALITY and
  FREEDOM OF CONSCIENCE

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 14/24: Democracy -- core idea, direct/representative, procedural/substantive
CORE IDEA -> "rule of the people", but in modern political philosophy it implies NOT ONLY
MAJORITY RULE but also POLITICAL EQUALITY, PARTICIPATION, ACCOUNTABILITY, PUBLIC JUSTIFICATION
and SOME PROTECTION OF RIGHTS
        |
        v
DISTINCTION 1 -- WHO ACTS?
  DIRECT DEMOCRACY .......... citizens DECIDE THEMSELVES
    STRENGTH: high participation, visible self-rule
    LIMIT: hard to SCALE in large modern states
  REPRESENTATIVE DEMOCRACY .. citizens ELECT rulers and lawmakers
    STRENGTH: workable for LARGE societies
    LIMIT: DISTANCE between people and power
  -> modern democracies are MAINLY REPRESENTATIVE, though REFERENDA, LOCAL ASSEMBLIES and
     PARTICIPATORY DEVICES preserve direct elements
        |
        v
DISTINCTION 2 -- WHAT IS BEING TESTED?
  PROCEDURAL DEMOCRACY ...... FOCUS: elections, competition, voting, rules
                              QUESTION: are rulers CHOSEN FAIRLY?
  SUBSTANTIVE DEMOCRACY ..... FOCUS: justice, rights, inclusion, REAL equality
                              QUESTION: do people ACTUALLY GOVERN under FAIR SOCIAL CONDITIONS?
  -> a polity may hold ELECTIONS yet remain deeply UNEQUAL, EXCLUSIONARY or MANIPULATIVE
  -> the AXIS OF DISPUTE: SCHUMPETER is the sharpest PROCEDURAL statement (Panel 20); the
     SUBSTANTIVE conception is its rival
        |
        v
REPRESENTATION IS NOT ONE THING -- FOUR STANDARD TOOLS
  DELEGATE ...... follows constituents' INSTRUCTIONS
  TRUSTEE ....... uses INDEPENDENT JUDGMENT
  MANDATE ....... authorises a STATED PARTY PROGRAMME
  DESCRIPTIVE vs SUBSTANTIVE -> WHO representatives ARE vs WHAT THEY DO for the represented
        |
        v
CONTROL -> neither pole is self-sufficient. A purely SUBSTANTIVE test licenses rule by those
  who claim to know what real equality requires; a purely PROCEDURAL test cannot distinguish a
  GENUINE election from a WELL-MANAGED one.
? RESIDUE -> the boundary between CONDITIONS of choice and OUTCOMES of past choices is not
  sharp, and no formulation has succeeded in fixing it
ANSWER USE -> say WHICH conception is in play in the FIRST THREE LINES of any democracy stem

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 15/24: Institutional and territorial design -- four axes, four trade-offs
STATUS -> these are SECONDARY FORM-AXES inside modern government. They do NOT replace the
printed MONARCHY - THEOCRACY - DEMOCRACY classification.
        |
        v
AXIS 1  PARLIAMENTARY <-------------------------------------------------> PRESIDENTIAL
  FUSION OF POWERS: the executive EMERGES FROM        SEPARATION OF POWERS: the executive has
  and REMAINS RESPONSIBLE TO the legislature          a SEPARATE electoral/constitutional
                                                      mandate and FIXED TENURE
  TRADE-OFF -> RESPONSIBILITY and REMOVABILITY vs STABILITY and possible DEADLOCK/
               PERSONALISATION
        |
AXIS 2  UNITARY <------------------------------------------------------------> FEDERAL
  sub-units exercise powers DELEGATED by the          constituent units AND centre hold
  CENTRE                                              CONSTITUTIONALLY DIVIDED competences
  TRADE-OFF -> UNIFORMITY and DECISIVENESS vs AUTONOMY, DIVERSITY-MANAGEMENT and a CHECK ON
               CONCENTRATION
        |
AXIS 3  FUSION <-------------------------------------------------> SEPARATION OF POWERS
  personnel and functions OVERLAP, especially         institutions receive DISTINCT competences
  executive/legislature                               and MUTUAL CHECKS
  TRADE-OFF -> COORDINATION vs PREVENTION OF ARBITRARY CONCENTRATION
        |
AXIS 4  CENTRALISATION <-----------------------------------------> DECENTRALISATION
  decisions CONCENTRATED at higher levels             authority exercised NEARER affected
                                                      communities under SUBSIDIARITY
  TRADE-OFF -> UNIFORM STANDARDS vs PARTICIPATION, LOCAL KNOWLEDGE and ACCOUNTABILITY
        |
        v
THE ACCOUNTABILITY CONTROL -- SAY THIS OR LOSE THE MARK
  FUSION need NOT mean ABSENCE OF CHECKS; SEPARATION need NOT produce PERFECT INDEPENDENCE.
  The question is whether each organ can EXPLAIN, CONTEST and CORRECT the others through
  workable CHECKS AND BALANCES WITHOUT PARALYSING government.
INDIA-FACING ILLUSTRATION -> PARLIAMENTARY government + HOLDING-TOGETHER FEDERAL structure
  under CONSTITUTIONAL SUPREMACY and JUDICIAL REVIEW; the Constitution (73rd and 74th
  Amendment) Acts, 1992, inserted PARTS IX and IX-A for RURAL and URBAN local government
  -> constitutional-STATUS illustration; NOT a claim that design BY ITSELF guarantees
     participation
TRAPS -> "parliamentary = democratic, presidential = authoritarian" | "federalism = democracy"
  | "separation = no institutional interaction". NO AXIS BY ITSELF proves a regime democratic.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 16/24: Liberal democracy and minority protection -- 2018 and 2020
WHAT LIBERAL DEMOCRACY IS -> POPULAR RULE + CONSTITUTIONAL LIMITS + RIGHTS + RULE OF LAW +
PROTECTION FOR MINORITIES
  IDEAL -> the majority governs WITHOUT CONVERTING NUMBER INTO UNRESTRICTED POWER, so that
           ELECTORAL DEFEAT REMAINS SURVIVABLE for those who lose

  * democracy WITHOUT rights becomes MAJORITARIANISM
  * citizenship requires EQUAL MORAL STANDING, not merely COUNTING HEADS
  * plural societies need CONSTITUTIONAL SAFEGUARDS for LANGUAGE, RELIGION, CULTURE, DISSENT
        |
        v

ACHIEVEMENTS (INSTITUTIONAL)                  LIMITS (SOCIAL)

  JUDICIAL REVIEW, civil liberties              ELECTORAL INCENTIVES may reward MAJORITARIAN

  and EXPRESSION                                minorities may be TOLERATED FORMALLY but
  possibility of CONSTITUTIONAL                 EXCLUDED SUBSTANTIVELY
  ACCOMMODATION
  ASYMMETRY -> achievements are INSTITUTIONAL and limits are SOCIAL, so the institutions may

        |

VERDICT -> liberal democracy is NORMATIVELY COMMITTED to minority protection, but its ACTUAL
SUCCESS depends on CONSTITUTIONAL CULTURE, SOCIAL EQUALITY and INSTITUTIONAL INDEPENDENCE
-> the honest answer to "how far" is CONDITIONALLY, AND UNEVENLY
        |
        v
[2018 Q1(a)] THE EXTRA MOVE -- "DEEPER PRINCIPLES FOR SOCIAL COHESION"
  RIGHTS are PROTECTIVE devices; protection is NOT ATTACHMENT
  COHESION SIDE -> FRATERNITY, EQUAL RESPECT, CONSTITUTIONAL MORALITY
  AMBEDKAR'S WARNING (the exact bridge) -> POLITICAL EQUALITY cannot long survive amidst deep
  SOCIAL and ECONOMIC INEQUALITY -> equal votes coexist with UNEQUAL VOICE
  CONCLUSION -> cohesion is a COMPLEMENT to rights, NEVER a SUBSTITUTE for them
COUNTERWEIGHTS (TOCQUEVILLE) -> ASSOCIATIONS, LOCAL SELF-GOVERNMENT, FREE PRESS and REVIEW are
  CONSTITUTIVE SAFEGUARDS, not anti-democratic obstacles
  ? LIMIT -> counter-majoritarian bodies require THEIR OWN ACCOUNTABILITY
OBJECTION/REPLY -> "limits on the majority are anti-democratic" / the reply is TEMPORAL:
  limits protect the CONDITIONS OF FUTURE MAJORITIES, including the losing side's chance to
  become one

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 17/24: Liberty, equality, participation and the distinctiveness test
THE TWO PRINCIPLES DEMOCRACY HOLDS TOGETHER
  LIBERTY .... citizens should be FREE to think, speak, associate and CHOOSE REPRESENTATIVES
  EQUALITY ... each citizen COUNTS AS ONE in political decision-making; NO NATURAL RULER
               EXISTS BY BIRTH
        |
        v
DEMOCRACY DOES NOT ABOLISH THE TENSION -- IT INSTITUTIONALISES IT
  too much LIBERTY without EQUALITY -> entrenches DOMINATION by WEALTH and STATUS
  too much EQUALITY without LIBERTY -> FLATTENS individuality and DISSENT
  -> a form of government claiming to have DISSOLVED the tension has SUPPRESSED one principle
        |
        v
THE DISTINCTIVENESS TEST -- THE MOVE MOST CANDIDATES MISS
  showing that democracy HAS liberty and equality is NOT ENOUGH
    a CONSTITUTIONAL MONARCHY may protect CIVIL LIBERTIES
    a BENEVOLENT ABSOLUTISM may treat subjects EQUALLY BEFORE THE LAW
  WHAT ONLY DEMOCRACY INSTITUTIONALISES
    * EQUAL AUTHORISATION of rule
    * PEACEFUL REPLACEMENT of rulers
  -> CONCEDE what rivals also secure; THEN name what they cannot. THAT is distinctiveness.
        |
        v
THE INDIAN BRIDGE -> AMBEDKAR: POLITICAL EQUALITY cannot long survive amidst deep SOCIAL and
ECONOMIC INEQUALITY -> the tension is not merely conceptual; it is a CONDITION OF SURVIVAL
        |
        v
PARTICIPATORY DEMOCRACY -- THE DEVELOPMENTAL ANSWER TO THIN VOTING
  CLAIM ..... citizens should NOT be confined to PERIODIC VOTING; real democratic life needs
              ACTIVE INVOLVEMENT in local institutions, workplaces, communities and PUBLIC
              DELIBERATION
  DEFENCE ... DEVELOPMENTAL -- participation EDUCATES citizens, generates CIVIC RESPONSIBILITY
              and RESISTS ALIENATION (MILL: participation improves PUBLIC CHARACTER)
  PATHOLOGY . PARTICIPATION FATIGUE | UNEQUAL VOICE | INSTABILITY through OVER-MOBILISATION
        |
        v
MILL'S RESIDUE, STATED HONESTLY ? -> representative government is valued for the DEVELOPMENTAL
  EDUCATION of citizens, but his COMPETENCE-WEIGHTING proposals show that democratic

HABERMAS -- THE DELIBERATIVE TURN
  SHIFT: from AGGREGATION of votes to PUBLIC REASONING
  LEGITIMACY GROWS where laws emerge through COMMUNICATION under conditions approximating

  it does NOT replace voting -- it DEEPENS it by asking whether citizens can JUSTIFY NORMS TO

THE EPISTEMIC DEFENCE -- THE REPLY TO PLATO, STATED CORRECTLY
  NOT "all opinions are equally true"
  BUT "democratic PROCEDURES allow ERROR-DETECTION, REVISION and broader access to DISPERSED
       SOCIAL KNOWLEDGE" -- institutions of DEBATE, ACCOUNTABILITY and CORRECTION may
       OUTPERFORM INSULATED ELITE RULE
        |
        v
EXPERTISE WITHOUT TECHNOCRACY -- FOUR STEPS
  1. complex administration REQUIRES specialised knowledge
  2. expertise does NOT decide value-conflicts; it clarifies CONSEQUENCES and FEASIBLE MEANS
  3. democratic institutions must AUTHORISE GOALS, DEMAND REASONS and HOLD EXPERTS ACCOUNTABLE
  4. the balanced model is EXPERTISE UNDER DEMOCRATIC CONTROL -- neither expertise replaced by
     polling nor democracy replaced by experts
THREE BOUNDED ANCHORS, ONE JOB EACH -> ROUSSEAU: why REPRESENTATION IS INCOMPLETE | MILL: why
  PARTICIPATION EDUCATES | TOCQUEVILLE: why MAJORITY RULE NEEDS COUNTERWEIGHTS

POWER ....... the ability to secure COMPLIANCE DESPITE RESISTANCE

THE RECONSTRUCTED ARGUMENT
  1. no regime can rely on COERCION ALONE -- surveillance costs rise WITHOUT LIMIT
  2. stability therefore requires that subjects BELIEVE the order is RIGHTFUL
  3. such beliefs take a LIMITED number of forms -- CUSTOM, PERSONAL DEVOTION, ENACTED RULE
  4. each form generates its OWN ADMINISTRATION and its OWN CHARACTERISTIC CRISIS
  5. therefore regimes are analysed by the TYPE OF LEGITIMACY CLAIM, not only by NUMBER
THE PRESUPPOSITION YOU MUST STATE
  legitimacy is a SOCIOLOGICAL FACT ABOUT BELIEF, not a NORMATIVE VERDICT
  the types describe what IS BELIEVED rightful; they do NOT certify what IS rightful
  x do NOT treat "legitimate" as equivalent to "justified" -- the COMMONEST MISUSE
ROUTINISATION OF CHARISMA -- THE BRIDGE BETWEEN THE TYPES
  charismatic authority DIES WITH THE LEADER -> must convert into TRADITIONAL or LEGAL-RATIONAL
  form to survive: HEREDITARY SUCCESSION | DESIGNATION | INSTITUTIONALISATION INTO OFFICE
  -> explains MOVEMENT -> PARTY -> BUREAUCRACY, and why hereditary monarchy is SYSTEMATIC
THE CROSSING THAT EARNS DEPTH -> the typology cuts ACROSS the classical classification: a
  monarchy may be traditional or charismatic; a theocracy joins traditional to charismatic; a
  democracy is normally LEGAL-RATIONAL but may be captured by a PLEBISCITARY-CHARISMATIC leader
  WHILE RETAINING ITS LEGAL FORMS
OBJECTIONS/REPLIES -> "ideal types explain nothing" / they are ANALYTICAL EXAGGERATIONS against
  which REAL MIXTURES are measured; the value is DIAGNOSTIC | "belief makes propaganda count as
  legitimate" / CORRECT, and that is a GENUINE LIMIT -- see Panel 22

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 20/24: Schumpeter and Michels -- the two objections that redefine democracy
SCHUMPETER -- DEMOCRACY AS A METHOD
  REJECTS the CLASSICAL DOCTRINE that democracy realises the WILL OF THE PEOPLE through
  representatives who execute it
  DEFINES democracy as the INSTITUTIONAL ARRANGEMENT in which individuals acquire the power to
  decide by means of a COMPETITIVE STRUGGLE FOR THE PEOPLE'S VOTE
  -> the people's function is to PRODUCE A GOVERNMENT, NOT TO GOVERN
  ARGUMENT: 1. the COMMON GOOD is not uniquely determinable | 2. no determinate WILL OF THE
  PEOPLE exists -- aggregate preferences are UNSTABLE and MANUFACTURED | 3. the ordinary
  citizen's grasp of REMOTE issues is WEAK | 4. therefore democracy cannot be defined by the
  CONTENT of the outcome | 5. it can be defined by a METHOD | 6. hence produce, not govern
  STRENGTHS -> EMPIRICALLY USABLE | distinguishes democracy from PLEBISCITARY ACCLAMATION |
  isolates the MINIMUM: an OPEN, UNCERTAIN, REPEATABLE contest
  OBJECTION -> DEFLATIONARY: citizenship becomes PERIODIC CHOICE AMONG ELITES; cannot
  distinguish a DELIBERATIVE election from a WELL-MANAGED one
  REPLY -> his is a MINIMUM, not a CEILING; participation WITHOUT competition can be MOBILISED
  ACCLAMATION. ? RESIDUE -> the minimum treated as the WHOLE definition licenses HOLLOW
  ELECTORALISM (Panel 21)
        |
        v
MICHELS -- THE ORGANISATIONAL OBJECTION (Political Parties, 1911)
  THE IRON LAW OF OLIGARCHY: every LARGE organisation -- INCLUDING one founded on explicitly
  DEMOCRATIC and EGALITARIAN aims, such as a socialist party or a trade union -- tends toward
  rule by a SMALL LEADERSHIP
  ARGUMENT: 1. large-scale ORGANISATION is indispensable for mass action | 2. it requires
  CONTINUITY, SPECIALISATION, TECHNICAL COMPETENCE and CONTROL OF INFORMATION | 3. only a
  FULL-TIME EXPERT LEADERSHIP can discharge these | 4. such leadership acquires
  IRREPLACEABILITY, controls COMMUNICATION, develops ITS OWN INTERESTS | 5. oligarchy emerges
  from the REQUIREMENTS OF ORGANISATION ITSELF, not from BAD FAITH | 6. "who says organisation
  says oligarchy"
  OBJECTION -> the "law" is NOT UNIFORM: organisations differ measurably, and rules on TERM
  LIMITS, INTERNAL ELECTIONS and TRANSPARENCY demonstrably ALTER OUTCOMES
  REPLY -> the defensible version is a RESISTIBLE TENDENCY, not a law: oligarchic drift is the
  DEFAULT and must be ACTIVELY RESISTED BY DESIGN. ? RESIDUE -> a tendency defeasible by design
  is NO LONGER AN *IRON* LAW
        |
        v
THE SEQUENCE TO STATE IN AN ANSWER -- THE DEPTH MARKER
  PLATO objects on grounds of COMPETENCE -> MICHELS objects on grounds of ORGANISATION ->
  SCHUMPETER concedes BOTH and REDEFINES democracy so that NEITHER objection is fatal
  running the three IN THAT ORDER converts a DESCRIPTIVE answer into an ARGUMENT
DEMOCRATIC-ELITIST REPLY -> what matters is whether elite selection is COMPETITIVE and
  ACCOUNTABLE -- which rescues democracy at the PRICE of conceding Schumpeter's DEFLATION

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 21/24: Populism, the slide to illiberal democracy, form against substance
POPULISM -- THREE NAMED ACCOUNTS, DELIBERATELY LEFT CONTESTED
  MUDDE, "The Populist Zeitgeist", Government and Opposition, 2004 -> a THIN-CENTRED IDEOLOGY
  dividing society into two homogeneous antagonistic camps, "the PURE PEOPLE" against "the
  CORRUPT ELITE"; being thin-centred it attaches to a HOST IDEOLOGY, hence LEFT, RIGHT,
  NATIONALIST and RELIGIOUS forms alike
  MULLER, What Is Populism?, 2016 -> the distinguishing feature is NOT anti-elitism (ordinary
  democratic politics) but the claim to EXCLUSIVE MORAL REPRESENTATION: only the populist
  represents the real people, so rivals are ENEMIES OF THE PEOPLE and dissenters are EXCLUDED
  FROM "the people" -> ANTI-ELITISM is compatible with democracy; ANTI-PLURALISM IS NOT
  LACLAU, On Populist Reason, 2005 -> a RIVAL account: populism as a LOGIC OF ARTICULATION
  linking disparate unsatisfied demands into a collective subject; potentially DEMOCRATISING
  where institutions EXCLUDE
  x do NOT present the three as ONE SETTLED DEFINITION. State the CONTEST, then adopt a
    position WITH REASONS.
THE SLIDE TO ILLIBERAL DEMOCRACY -- SIX STEPS
  1. a genuine REPRESENTATION DEFICIT exists | 2. a leader ARTICULATES the excluded demands and
  claims to EMBODY the real people | 3. because "the people" is defined MORALLY, not
  procedurally, DISAGREEMENT BECOMES BETRAYAL | 4. checking institutions -- courts, second
  chambers, commissions, free press, federal units -- are recast as ELITE OBSTRUCTIONS |
  5. they are WEAKENED, CAPTURED or BYPASSED while ELECTIONS CONTINUE | 6. result: an electoral
  majority governs WITHOUT the constraints that make ELECTORAL DEFEAT SURVIVABLE
  CONTROLLING DISTINCTION -> MAJORITY RULE is a DECISION PROCEDURE; CONSTITUTIONAL DEMOCRACY is
  majority rule PLUS entrenched rights, independent adjudication and SECURED CONDITIONS FOR THE
  NEXT CONTEST
FORM RETAINED / SUBSTANCE ERODED -- THE 20-MARK GRID
  elections continue ...... periodic contest ..... FAIRNESS of contest: access, finance, media,
                                                   adjudication of disputes
  a majority governs ...... majority rule ........ LIMITS on what a majority may do to minorities
  courts function ......... judicial machinery ... INDEPENDENCE of appointment, tenure,
                                                   enforcement
  a press exists .......... formal freedom ....... PLURALITY of ownership, absence of indirect
                                                   pressure
  opposition exists ....... multiparty form ...... REALISTIC PROSPECT OF ALTERNATION IN POWER
  READ THE GRID AS A DIAGNOSTIC -> it names WHAT WOULD HAVE TO BE SHOWN, and BY WHAT EVIDENCE
OBJECTION/REPLY -> "populism is a SMEAR applied by incumbents to any mass challenge" / concede
  the danger, then apply the MULLER CRITERION: the test is not whether a movement is ANTI-ELITE
  or DISRUPTIVE, but whether it claims EXCLUSIVE representation and DENIES the legitimacy of
  opposition -- a criterion applicable WITHOUT REGARD TO PROGRAMME
OBJECTION/REPLY -> "this protects UNELECTED institutions against DEMOCRATIC MAJORITIES" / the
  reply is TEMPORAL: constitutional limits protect the CONDITIONS OF FUTURE MAJORITIES,
  including the LOSING SIDE'S ability to BECOME a majority. A majority that removes those
  conditions ABOLISHES THE MECHANISM by which it could itself later be replaced.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 22/24: Propaganda, manufactured consent and the regulator's dilemma
WHY THIS IS MORE THAN "MISINFORMATION IS BAD"
  WEBER'S typology makes legitimacy a matter of BELIEF; Panel 21 shows that belief can be
  ENGINEERED. Together they give the PHILOSOPHICAL FORM of the propaganda problem.
THE RECONSTRUCTED ARGUMENT -- SIX STEPS
  1. democratic AUTHORISATION is valuable because it expresses the JUDGMENT OF CITIZENS
  2. a judgment is the citizen's OWN only if formed under ACCESS to relevant information,
     EXPOSURE to rival argument and FREEDOM FROM MANIPULATION
  3. concentrated communication power, AGENDA CONTROL, emotional saturation, repetition and
     TARGETED DISINFORMATION degrade ALL THREE conditions
  4. consent so produced is MANUFACTURED, NOT GIVEN
  5. an election may generate legitimacy in WEBER'S SOCIOLOGICAL sense while FAILING to
     generate AUTHORISATION in the NORMATIVE sense
  6. therefore democratic quality depends on the CONDITIONS OF OPINION-FORMATION, not only on
     the COUNTING OF VOTES
  PRESUPPOSITION -> citizens have a capacity for AUTONOMOUS JUDGMENT that manipulation can
  DEGRADE -- an EMPIRICAL premise, and one an answer should STATE rather than ASSUME
THE REGULATOR'S DILEMMA -- THE WHOLE 2022 MARK
  remedies against propaganda -- RESTRICTIONS ON FALSE SPEECH, CONTROL OF PLATFORMS,
  LICENSING -- HAND THE STATE POWER OVER PUBLIC TRUTH, which is the VERY POWER a
  propagandising state ABUSES
  -> DEFENSIBLE REMEDIES ARE STRUCTURAL, NOT CONTENT-BASED
       PLURAL OWNERSHIP | TRANSPARENCY of political finance and paid political messaging |
       INDEPENDENT ELECTORAL ADJUDICATION | PROTECTED PROFESSIONAL JOURNALISM |
       CIVIC EDUCATION | RIGHTS OF REPLY
  -> an answer that ends at "REGULATE FAKE NEWS" HAS NOT SEEN THE PROBLEM
THE CONSTRUCTIVE HALF -> HABERMAS: legitimacy grows where laws emerge through COMMUNICATION
  approximating FREEDOM, RECIPROCITY and ABSENCE OF DOMINATION -- deliberation DEEPENS voting
  rather than replacing it, and is the standing reply to POLARISATION and SHALLOW MAJORITARIANISM
INDIAN APPLICATION -- DATED, CLASSIFIED, NON-EMPIRICAL
  ARTICLE 19(1)(a) guarantees FREEDOM OF SPEECH AND EXPRESSION, SUBJECT TO REASONABLE
  RESTRICTIONS under ARTICLE 19(2) on specified grounds -> a constitutional provision defining
  a PERMISSIBLE ZONE OF RESTRICTION, NOT a philosophical resolution of the dilemma
  the REPRESENTATION OF THE PEOPLE ACT, 1951, is an ENACTED STATUTE governing the conduct of
  elections, including CORRUPT PRACTICES and DISQUALIFICATIONS
  -> ENACTMENT IS NOT ENFORCEMENT, and a statute cannot establish that OPINION-FORMATION IS IN
     FACT FREE
  x NO Indian party, government, leader or period is described as POPULIST, ILLIBERAL or
    PROPAGANDIST. Where a stem invites contemporary comment, answer AT THE LEVEL OF CRITERIA.
OBJECTION/REPLY -> "if preferences are MANUFACTURED, elite competition is a contest to
  manufacture them BETTER, so democracy has no defence" / the objection assumes the conditions
  CANNOT BE REPAIRED; they can be addressed STRUCTURALLY, though NEVER ELIMINATED

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 23/24: The six pathologies and the eight comparison axes
THE SIX RECURRING DEMOCRATIC PATHOLOGIES -- MECHANISM | REPLY | RESIDUE
  TYRANNY OF THE MAJORITY .. number becomes unlimited authority | entrenched rights,
    independent review, federal/consociational protection | checks look like OBSTRUCTION
  ELITE CAPTURE ............ organisation, money, expertise, information concentrate influence
    | competition, transparency, internal democracy, alternation | counter-elites CIRCULATE
  SHORT-TERMISM ............ electoral incentives privilege immediate benefit | scrutiny,
    independent institutions, intergenerational duties | INSULATION weakens control
  POPULIST ANTI-PLURALISM .. exclusive moral representation | protected opposition, plural
    media, fair future contest | "populism" becomes a PARTISAN LABEL
  MANUFACTURED CONSENT ..... agenda control degrades opinion-formation | structural plurality,
    transparency, civic education | CONTENT REGULATION empowers the state over TRUTH
  BUREAUCRATIC DOMINATION .. expertise and files displace representative control | reasons,
    review, oversight, decentralisation | complex government NEEDS specialised administration
        |
        v
THE EIGHT COMPARISON AXES -- RUN ALL THREE FORMS DOWN EACH, NEVER THREE MINI-ESSAYS
  1 SOURCE OF AUTHORITY | 2 TYPE OF LEGITIMACY CLAIMED (Weber) | 3 MODE OF SUCCESSION OR
  SELECTION | 4 ACCOUNTABILITY | 5 RELATION TO LAW | 6 TREATMENT OF DISSENT | 7 EQUALITY OF
  CITIZENSHIP | 8 CAPACITY TO CORRECT ERROR
  -> AXIS 8 most often DECIDES the verdict and is the one candidates most often OMIT
  MONARCHY on axis 8 -> prudent counsel and customary restraint; NO regular non-violent
    procedure for acting on the finding that a ruler is incompetent
  THEOCRACY on axis 8 -> hardest: the norm is UNAMENDABLE and the interpreter INSULATED, so
    dissent is RECLASSIFIED rather than answered
  DEMOCRACY on axis 8 -> institutionalised through OPPOSITION, ELECTIONS, COURTS and PUBLIC
    CRITICISM -- but CONDITIONAL, since form can survive substance
COMPARE/DISTINGUISH RULE -> run the SAME AXES in PARALLEL down all three forms. NEVER write
  three disconnected mini-essays. AXIS 8 is the one candidates most often OMIT and the one
  that most often DECIDES the verdict.
THE DIRECTIVE DECODER -- THE VERB FIXES THE STRUCTURE
  COMMENT ON ............. claim -> argument -> ONE objection -> verdict, in that order
  DISCUSS ................ doctrine -> rival -> objection -> reply -> verdict
  CRITICALLY EXAMINE ..... the OBJECTION-REPLY LAYER *IS* the answer: two objections, each
                           with a reply and a RESIDUAL PROBLEM
  CAN X BE ACCEPTED? ..... FIX THE TEST FIRST (consent, accountability, equal citizenship,
                           error-correction) -> apply -> verdict
  DOES X LEAVE ROOM FOR Y? show what X ENTAILS, then what Y REQUIRES, then whether they can
                           CO-EXIST -- conceptual, not a list of historical examples
  HOW FAR ARE X AND Y      show what Z has that RIVALS LACK; CONCEDE what rivals also secure
  DISTINCTIVE OF Z? .....
  COMPARE / DISTINGUISH .. fix 5-8 shared AXES, run all forms down each, THEN adjudicate

ANSWER METHODS -- MOVES AND MINUTES
  10 MARKS (~150-220 words, 4 moves, ~12 min) -> EXACT TARGET -> ARGUMENT SPINE as numbered
    premises -> ONE evidence unit WITH ITS LIMITATION -> GRADED VERDICT, usually ASYMMETRIC
    x do NOT run the authority typology, the populism debate and the classical grid together
  15 MARKS (~250-330 words, 6 moves, ~18 min) -> FRAME THE AXIS OF DISPUTE -> strongest case
    FOR, stated charitably -> strongest case AGAINST on the same axes -> ONE TABLE -> ONE fully
    worked OBJECTION -> REPLY -> RESIDUAL PROBLEM -> CONDITIONAL verdict naming what survives
  20 MARKS (~330-400 words, 8 moves, ~25 min) -> PROVISIONAL THESIS using the stem's own verb ->
    CLASSIFICATORY PLACEMENT on BOTH grids -> strongest case FOR with its PRESUPPOSITION ->
    strongest RIVAL -> TWO objection-reply chains each ending in a RESIDUAL PROBLEM -> the
    FORM/SUBSTANCE or ROUTINISATION analysis -> ONE DATED INDIAN ILLUSTRATION, classified ->
    GRADED VERDICT conceding something to the losing side
        |
        v
GRADED VERDICT FORMULAS -- ADAPT, NEVER REPRODUCE MECHANICALLY
  ASYMMETRIC (Plato) -> "the diagnosis of demagoguery and defective leadership-selection
    survives; the authoritarian remedy does not, for want of consent and external correction"
  DISTINCTIVENESS (2018) -> "liberty and equality are not unique in FORMAL STATEMENT, but
    democracy alone institutionalises EQUAL AUTHORISATION and PEACEFUL REPLACEMENT"
  CRITERION (theocracy) -> "judged by consent, equal citizenship and revisability, defective;
    judged by moral limitation of the ruler, it identifies a real deficiency"
  FORM/SUBSTANCE -> "the regime retains democratic FORM; the question is whether it retains the
    CONDITIONS that make the form worth having"
  CROSS-TYPOLOGY -> "classification by NUMBER tells us who decides; classification by
    LEGITIMACY tells us why they are obeyed. A complete assessment requires BOTH."
  DILEMMA (propaganda) -> "democracy survives propaganda through CONTESTABLE INSTITUTIONS
    rather than CENSORSHIP, because every content-based remedy hands the state precisely the
    power over public truth that a propagandising state abuses"
```
